ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u88853	Thank you very much, Mr. President of the Croatian Parliament, Mr. Vice President, ladies and gentlemen of Parliament. Here is the Ministry of Construction and Spatial Planning amending the Law on Renting Apartments aims in a fair way to solve the problem of decades old. The proposed amendments to the Law on the Rental of dwellings of the Republic of Croatia address the problem of many decades old in a fair and sustainable way. This achieves a balance between the opposing interests of the tenants and the tenants and the owners of the apartments, i.e. the tenants, receive the exact day when they can freely dispose of their real estate and since then gradually increase their protected rent by reducing the gap compared to the market rent. The date will take place on 1 September 2023. In addition to 5 years of retaining a protected tenant's status, protected tenants also receive a state subsidy when paying the market rent after the expiry of the existing status for an additional 5 years. It will also have an advantage in buying or renting POS apartments as well as an advantage in subsidised loans and an advantage in applications for accommodation in nursing homes. Was it easy to make decisions regarding this law? Surely it is not because it would not have waited 20 years to remove the shortcomings of Croatian legislation and to fill a legal vacuum. We're going to do this because we want to correct an injustice that's been going on for decades, in some cases over 60 years. Croatia must and Croatia will be a social state and continue to take care of protected tenants but not on the burden of citizens and their private ownership. These amendments to the law will remove the shortcomings of Croatian legislation established by the European Court of Human Rights in the Statileo case against the Republic of Croatia relating to the inadequate level of protected rent, restrictive conditions for the cancellation of the protected lease and the absence of a time limit on the relation to the system of protected rental. The Ministry of Construction and Spatial Planning will finally determine the fairer fees and timing in which owners will finally exercise their right to property. It is our intention to ensure a balance between the interests of owners, employers, including their right to profit from their assets and the general interest of the community, including the availability of satisfactory accommodation for people in a worse position and all in accordance with the principles of the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms. The proposed law also fills in the legal gap created after the decision of the Constitutional Court of 1998, which concerns the cancellation of the lease agreement and insurance to the tenant of the second appropriate apartment. Furthermore, in the case of Statileo v Croatia, the European Court of Human Rights analysed in detail the domestic legal rules relating to the former tenants' rights and derived from them the status of protected tenants and on 10 July 2014 reached a verdict in the case which became final on 10 October 2014. The European Court of Human Rights found that the existing legislative framework governing the issue of protected rental of dwellings in the Republic of Croatia did not establish an appropriate balance between the opposing interests of the owners of the apartments in which protected tenants, the interest of the state to ensure the implementation of housing policy, the reform of the housing sector after the fall of the Socialist system. The European Court of Human Rights has concluded that the applicable legislation of the Republic of Croatia, in particular the House Rental Act, imposes an excessive individual burden on residents because they are forced to bear most of the social and financial costs of housing of a protected tenant and that Article 1, Protocol 1, in addition to the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms, is injured. Accordingly, the Republic of Croatia has been ordered to pay the heir to the complaint of material damage, immaterial damages and costs. In addition, while respecting the fact that the breach of the Convention in this case is caused by the content of domestic legislation which is not in accordance with the Convention, the Republic of Croatia must take concrete legislative measures in the process of executing the judgment against Croatia which will address the problems of continuing the excessive burden imposed on the owners of apartments in which protected tenants live and which will prevent further identical infringements of the Convention in the domestic legal system. In this respect, the Republic of Croatia should ensure a balance between the interests of the employers, including their right to obtain profits from their assets and the general interest of the Community, including the availability of satisfactory accommodation for persons in a more vulnerable position in accordance with the principles of protection of property rights under the Convention. In this respect, the European Court of Human Rights has identified 3 main shortcomings in existing legislation and these are inadequate levels of the protected amount of rent in terms of legal financial burdens imposed on the tenants, restrictive conditions for the cancellation of the protected lease and the absence of any time limit in relation to the protected rental system. Following the judgment in the case of Statileo, the European Court of Human Rights has today reached the same verdicts in the case of Anzulović-Mirosevic, Bego, Bulic, Doris Knego, Ingrid Knego and Matas. Also, after the judgment mentioned, a number of tenants in which the protected tenants filed charges before the courts of the Republic of Croatia seeking compensation for the material damage suffered as a result of the violation of their right to the peaceful enjoyment of their ownership. For the purpose of finding the best way to fulfil the obligations arising from those judgments of the European Court of Human Rights under the available budget from units of local self-government, the Association of Owners of dwellings in which protected tenants live and the Association of protected tenants have requested data on the number of private-owned dwellings in which protected tenants and other related data live. However, the largest number of units of local self-government and those associations did not provide the requested data. According to official DZS data obtained on 18 April 2018, which were collected on the last official census in 2011 in the Republic of Croatia, 3726 were privately owned in which protected tenants live. Taking into account the fact that according to official data from the DZS collected on the last list in the Republic of Croatia, 1600 tenants older than 60 years were protected, the options for the housing of older protected tenants were also considered and the number of homes for older and powerless persons was established. The reasons why we decided to make and make one more amendment and the addition of the law are the following. The adoption of the law in question will bring into line the criteria for the insurance of the second habitable apartment when the apartment is provided by the tenant, the owner of the apartment with the criteria for the insurance of the second habitable apartment when the apartment is provided by the local self-government unit, thus fulfilling the obligation laid down in the Constitutional Court decision of the Republic of Croatia since 31 March 1998, to fill the legal gap that causes the problems or inability to exercise the rights of the tenants, owners and protected tenants prescribed by the Law on Renting of Apartments. In addition, the adoption of the law in question will create a legal framework for the establishment of an appropriate balance between the opposing interests of the owners of dwellings in which protected tenants live, the interest of the State to ensure the implementation of the housing policy so that the owners of the dwellings are gradually able to obtain their property and make available fully available to the tenants on 1 September 2023, while at the same time protecting the general interest of the community which, together with the above, consists of ensuring the availability of satisfactory accommodation for persons in a worse position and ensuring the rights of the tenants, residents to respect their private and family life and at home guaranteed by the provisions of Article 8. Convention. In this way, the Republic of Croatia will fully fulfil its international obligations stemming from the European Court of Human Rights ruling in the Statileo case against Croatia. What was the procedure? At the 7th session held on 2 March 2018, the Croatian Parliament discussed in its first reading a proposal for a Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Rent of Apartments, which it concluded with the conclusion that all comments, proposals and opinions were sent to the applicant in order to draw up a final draft of the law. The following issues are covered by the subject-law. The criteria of the size and location of the second habitable apartment are evend in case of cancellation of the lease agreement for an indefinite period of time in which the apartment is required, the apartment is obliged to provide the owner of the apartment with criteria in which the second residential apartment is obliged to provide the unit of local self-government or the city of Zagreb in such a way that it is necessary to provide the appropriate apartment, where the apartment is considered to be located in the same municipality or city of the size of one person – one room, with the number of rooms not greater than the number of rooms in the apartment from which it is moved. Legal protection of the tenant and the protected tenant shall be governed in the event of the cancellation of the lease agreement and in the case where the unit of local self-government is obliged to provide the protected tenant with another suitable apartment with the rights and obligations of the protected tenant. The termination of the right of the tenant and protected tenant to the protected rent and other rights of protected tenants and protected tenants on the date of entry into force of this Act, if he, his spouse, life partner or member of the family household which the tenant is obliged to maintain in the territory of the Republic of Croatia, has the property of a habitable apartment or house. A gradual increase in the amount of protected rent from 1 September 2018 to 31 August 2023 in dwellings not owned by units of local self-government, regional or regional self-government or the Republic of Croatia, and the mechanism of payment of the difference between increased rent and protected rent by the Republic of Croatia and the unit of local self-government for socially sensitive groups of tenants, i.e. for users of dwellings who are members of the family of the mortally injured or missing Croatian defender from the Homeland War or of the Croatian War Disabled from the Homeland War and are not residently cared for by those residents who receive permanent social assistance under a special regulation. Discontinuation of the right of tenants and protected tenants to the protected rent and other rights of protected tenants and protected tenants except for the right of first purchases of apartments that are not owned by units of local self-government, regional self-government or the Republic of Croatia on 1 September 2023. The housing status of persons who, on the date of entry into force of the House Rental Act, have ceased residential law and persons who have ceased the status of protected tenants in an apartment not owned by a local self-government unit, a regional self-government unit or a Republic of Croatia, who have not entered into a lease agreement under the House Rental Act and have not requested a court ruling to replace that contract. The right to subsidise the freely contracted rent of the tenant and protected tenants whose net income per household member has ceased to be less than or equal to half of the average salary in the Republic of Croatia for the past year and during the period from 1 September 2023 to 31 August 2028 shall be laid down. The advantage of protected tenants and protected tenants of dwellings not owned by units of local self-government, units of regional self-government or of the Republic of Croatia to other interested persons when renting or selling apartments carried out under rules governing socially encouraged housing, and apartments owned by units of local self-government, units of regional and regional self-government or of the Republic of Croatia which are leased or sold through a public tender shall be prescribed. It provides for the possibility of the tenant, including the Republic of Croatia of the local self-government unit, to use this right to fulfil its obligation, to ensure an appropriate apartment in the event of the cancellation of the lease contract and to introduce a measure to ensure the construction of the necessary number of apartments under the social incentive scheme for the rental or purchase of all interested protected tenants, protected tenants and pre-rented tenants. The final proposal of the Law on Amendments and Supplements to the House Rental Act differs from the proposal of that Act in the following. The date from which the protected rent starts to be increased from 1 July to 1 September 2018 and the date of termination of the right to the protected rent from 1 July to 1 September 2023 was changed. The advantage of interested protected tenants and protected tenants of dwellings not owned by units of local self-government, the unit of regional self-government or the Republic of Croatia to other interested persons in the RS credit subsidisation programmes taken from credit institutions for the purchase of an apartment or house or the construction of a house in order to resolve their residential issue shall be prescribed. A measure shall be introduced to ensure the construction of the necessary number of dwellings under the Socially Incentived Rent Programme, i.e. the purchase of all interested protected tenants, protected tenants and pre-edited tenants, which shall be notified within the set time limit with a request for the same. The advantage of interested tenants and protected tenants who have reached 70 years of age, dwellings not owned by units of local self-government or the Republic of Croatia over other interested persons when receiving a home for older and powerless persons whose founder is a local self-government unit in the area of the local self-government unit in which he resides or in the area of another unit of local self-government. The right to professional assistance of protected tenants, protected tenants and pre-random tenants and persons who have ceased the status of protected tenants and protected tenants in finding and intermediation in the conclusion of an apartment or house purchase contract in the area of units of local self-government in which they reside or in the area of another unit of local self-government shall be prescribed. The necessary resources from the state budget, according to the information provided in the first reading of this law in the State budget of the Republic of Croatia, are necessary for the subsidisation of freely agreed rents from 1 September '23. until 31 August '28. provide approximately 9.5 million kuna for a period of 12 months a year and a total of 6 years 47.5 million kuna. That amount is significantly subject to a possible gradual reduction, depending on the extent to which the Republic of Croatia or the units of local and regional (regional) self-government will ensure the use of apartments owned or owned by local self-government units. And lastly, I need to point out again why we're going into these amendments to the law. These amendments to the law of the Republic of Croatia solve the problem of a legal gap that has been in existence for 20 years, and we will resolve this in a fair and sustainable manner. This means that we've been correcting an injustice that's been going on for decades. These amendments to the laws strike a balance between the conflicting interests of the tenants and the tenants. The owners of the apartments receive the exact date when they can fully and freely dispose of their real estate. The date will be held on 1 September 2023. It is these amendments that we are harmonising the law with the highest courts, such as the European Court of Human Rights and our constitutional court. This puts an end to potential lawsuits and damages because we permanently resolve the housing issue to the tenants and enter into possession of the employers. The state will take care of protected tenants, but not private property of citizens, but of its own resources. Thank you.

It is pointed out that despite the application of the social system, the protected tenants were not illegally moving into private apartments, but they were also holders of residential rights as well as those tenants in social housing but did not have the possibility of buying the apartment because the apartment is privately owned. In the year, a new legal arrangement of housing relations was created, leaving the legal concept of household law and allowing tenants to become protected tenants. So on the one hand, we have owners of apartments that can't be used and dispose of their property, and on the other hand, tenants who stayed in private apartments as tenants are later protected tenants, and they didn't have the possibility of buying a private apartment like those tenants in social housing. We all know the '96 renting law. it was not the happiest solution and was subject to an assessment of compliance with the Constitution of the Republic of Croatia, which, at the time of the Constitutional Court, abolished certain provisions of the law. At the same time, the European Court of Human Rights in the case of Statileo v Croatia found that Croatia's legislative framework did not strike a balance between the interests of the owners of dwellings in which protected tenants and the interests of the state live to ensure the implementation of housing policy. He identified the main shortcomings, which is an inadequate level of protected rent, conditions for cancellation of the protected lease and the absence of any time limit in relation to the secure rental system. On that occasion, the Court decided that the Republic of Croatia should take appropriate legislative and other general measures to ensure a balance between the interests of the employer, including their right to gain from its assets and the general interest of the community. And that's why it was necessary to start solving this problem. We are aware that in a situation like this, it is difficult to find a solution in which we would all be completely satisfied and that this bill is just the beginning of resolving them. And certainly not the only legal framework in solving this situation. I must remind once again that the protected tenants 50 or some even 70 years lived in apartments that did not belong to them and paid a symbolic rent. All this time, the owners of these apartments were unable to use their properties. We have various examples, we have an example where apartment of 40 square meters in the strict center of Split is protected by the tenant pay 250 kuna per month, and 250 kuna is the very reserve paid by the owner. We have examples where we really need help from these protected tenants who can't handle this issue on their own, and it's precisely this law that regulates the involvement of the Republic of Croatia, the unit of local self-government that will find the most optimal solution for everyone. These amendments to the law of the Republic of Croatia address the problem of legal voids that have existed for 20 years in a fair and sustainable way. This means that these changes are corrected by an injustice that has lasted for 7 decades, a balance is reached between the opposing interests of the tenant and the tenant. ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89147	And now we move to the next item of the agenda - Proposal for a Law on State Measurement and Real Estates The Proposer is the Government of the Republic of Croatia on the basis of Article 85. The Constitution of the Republic of Croatia and Article 72 Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament. When discussing this item of the agenda, the provisions of the Rules of Procedure relating to first reading of the law shall apply. The debate was conducted by the Committee on Legislative Affairs and the Committee on Spatial Planning and Construction. Does the representative of the motionor, or Mr. Secretary of State, Željko Uhlir, want to have an opening statement? Yes, go ahead.
ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89148	Thank you very much. Dear ladies and gentlemen, the Honorable Chairman. The proposal of the State Measurement Act and the real estate catastro is the last time, has been in place for 10 years with 6 amendments. Meanwhile, new requirements of the real estate market and the resolution of property-legal relations have emerged in order to create preconditions for new investment and development of the economy and to develop technologies in the area of access and use of data to the national measurement of real estate catasters. The specific issues covered by the law relate to the state measure, the real estate cadastre, the infrastructure catalogue, the building register, the spatial unit register, the geographical name register, the jurisdiction and the performance of those transactions and the storage of the use of the state measurement data and the real estate catalogue. The adoption of a new Law on State Measurement and Real Estate Catastrophe shall establish or improve records and registers from the jurisdiction of the State Geodesy Administration, improve actions within the administrative field of state measurement and real estate catasters, the common information system of land books and catastras and electronic operations. It shall also ensure faster and more efficient access and use of up-to-date data from the jurisdiction of the State Geodetic Authority. The law prescribes matters of state measurement from basic geodetic works, topographic measurement and production of state maps and measurement and labelling and keeping records of the state border on land. The establishment of a basic topographic base will ensure the production and maintenance of official state maps as well as the use and use of data to produce other official cartographic depictions. In addition, the area of the real estate cadastre is prescribed and until the production of a property cadastre in certain catastrian municipalities, i.e. to the subsequent translation of cadastrian particles into the real estate cadastre, the land cadastre will be maintained in a way that gradually adapts to the catastrophe of real estate. For those catastrian municipalities and cadastrian particles on which the relevant pre-conditions prescribed by this cadastrian congratulatory law will be brought individually from land cadastra to cadastrian real estate particles. The planning of operations in the field of real estate is planned through a multi-annual programme brought by the Croatian Parliament, which is proposed by the Government of the Republic of Croatia by the State Geodetic Administration, with the agreement of the Minister in charge of Justice Affairs, which will ensure the restoration of the cadastrian opera and land books with an emphasis on extended construction areas. The quality of field measurement data, hunting points, borders and other limits of cadastar particles and buildings and other buildings will be ensured with a standard of accuracy of up to 0,1 metres. This contributes to the introduction of an increase in security in data that are part of the official records of the real estate catastra. The procedures in the catastrian measurement have been amended by introducing the process and presenting the data presented in the catalogue of the cadastral measurement to the persons shown in the elaborate, which will result in the acceleration of public-examination procedures, data collected by the cadastral measurement, and which is carried out by the Commission of the State Geodetic Administration at the same time as the procedure for establishing, renewing and supplementing the land book. The same will affect citizens, entrepreneurs, farmers and all other land administration users, thus speeding up and making it more efficient to put into use cadastrian operations and land books. In order to improve the quality of existing cadastrian plans drawn up by graphic measurement methods, the process of homogenising the cadastrian plans is carried out, which is a technical process to implement the positional improvement of the cadastrian plans and to correct the internal inhomogenicities of the Kadastrian plan and which has no impact on the data shown in the list-book part of the Kadastrian operat. Further maintenance of the common information system of land books and the catastra is prescribed popularly called ZIS, which is already in use in all catastrian offices. Persons who have the consent to carry out expert geodetic activities, the maintenance of catastras, land and real estate can apply in accordance with specific law for confirmation of geodetic elaborate by digital means, with the use of digital elaborates, which will speed up the review and verification of the elaborates. The register of buildings will be established as a record of all buildings and special parts of buildings built in the area of the Republic of Croatia and as such will have a spatial component. For the purpose of establishing a register of buildings, data from real estate, state measurements, register of spatial units, land books, records of units of local and regional (regional) self-government, data from works for construction according to specific regulations in the field of spatial planning and construction, and records kept by building managers and from other sources shall be downloaded and used. The jurisdiction of the State Geodetic Authority for the determination of home numbers is prescribed, with units of local self-government which have so far established and carried out by law conferred the power to continue these tasks. The competent authority of the State Geodesy Administration for the establishment, management and maintenance of the infrastructure catastras as well as the availability of infrastructure data and information on ongoing and planned construction works for the entire Republic of Croatia, which will establish a single and comprehensive record for the whole country. The units of local self-government that have established and implemented these jobs so far have been passed over to the authorities to continue to carry out these tasks. The improvement of the register of spatial units as one of the main registers creates preconditions for more efficient management and for the exchange and use of data registers, spatial units by other state bodies and entities. By improving the register of geographical names, the system of collecting geographical names will be upgraded, resulting in greater availability and eased use of those spatial data by other national authorities and other users. The law establishes an interdisciplinary body responsible for the standardisation of geographical names which will have the responsibility and competence of professional management of the problem of geographical names in accordance with international standards. The law lays down, the law regulates the conduct of electronic business for the publication of data from the records and documentation of the state geodetic administration, the availability of data through network services, which allows easier and more efficient access to data from jurisdiction. It is prescribed that the data from the jurisdiction are public unless a certain restriction is prescribed by some specific law. The creation of a digital archive system will solve the problem of difficult access to archive documentation of state measurement and real estate catastra and ensure that the data documentation from the jurisdiction is kept permanently. The translation of analogue construction into digital form is carried out in conversion centres at the State Geodetic Administration. Finally, the new law will achieve the following positive effects, ensure the achievement of the framework for the establishment of a central system for the construction, storage and distribution and data management of the infrastructure catalogue for the entire Republic of Croatia, the creation of a framework for the establishment of a register of buildings and parts of buildings, the creation of a framework for the improvement of the common information system of land books and catasters, the improvement of the register of spatial units, the improvement of the register of geographical names, the revision of business processes in catasters and teaching in land books in order to significantly reduce the number of procedures, the avoidance of double implementations resulting from current rules in catasterical and land book procedures. In the domain of cadastrian measurements, improvements are planned to affect citizens, entrepreneurs, farmers and all other land administration users, thus speeding up and making the process of putting them into use more efficiently. Furthermore, faster access to data will be ensured to improve the digital archive system and translation of existing analogue documentation into digital format. Normally, the process of issuing data from official catastra records will be regulated in accordance with the Government of the Republic of Croatia's programme, the programme for the development of electronic services and e-Citizen projects via the Internet. Finally, the law improves the general processes of national measurement and real estate catastra and will achieve all the positive effects provided for in this law. Thank you very much.

As land books are known to represent the legal state of real estate in the Republic of Croatia and real and other relevant real estate rights are entered in the land books. In view of the public principle and the principle of trust and truth in the fullness of content data, land books protect fundamental property rights. Until now, when a user needed installation data, he had to ask for 4 to 5 or more public, private data and visit several places, which is unnecessary spending of funds and time. ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89181	This law on the municipal economy was passed the first time in 1995 and has been amended 18 times to date. This law regulates the principles, manner and implementation of the financing of the municipal economy, as well as other issues relating to the purpose of carrying out municipal activities in which the concept of a municipal economy involves the pursuit of municipal activities and, in particular, the provision of municipal services of interest to natural and legal persons, and the financing of construction and maintenance of facilities, public infrastructure devices as a whole system in the area, municipalities, cities, cities and the county when determined by that law. The unusually large number of amendments and additions make up the text of the applicable Law on the Communist Economy extremely non-transparent and difficult to achieve in the implementation. This is further contributed by the fact that in 6 cases the Law on the municipal economy has been amended by other laws and, in particular, by the fact that these laws are more or less parallel to the issues related to certain municipal infrastructure, its construction and financing of its construction, and by three of these laws, by the Water Act, the Law on Sustainable Waste Management and the Law on burial activity, certain businesses have ceased to be a communal activity within the meaning of the Law on the Communist Economy. In more than 20 years, the implementation of the Law on the Communist Economy has seen numerous other shortcomings in that law that could not be removed by its many amendments and additions. These shortcomings are seen mainly as legal gaps, for example, when it comes to building the property's legal status of municipal infrastructure, organisational forms, carrying out municipal activity, municipal order and the work of municipal directors, and as relatively poor, insufficient, or inadequately regulated individual issues. The secret concerns, above all, the issue of principles on which the municipal economy must rest, which are insufficiently arranged and the municipal contribution and the communal compensation, which is the basis and most important sources of funding for the construction of the municipal infrastructure, which needs to be regulated, in a way that will remove identified shortcomings and without increasing the financial burden on the taxpayers to pay them. In addition, certain issues related to the organisational forms of carrying out municipal activity specifically, with a company carrying out municipal activity, a public institution and the confidence of the provision of municipal services to a contract, were to be brought into line with the Public Procurement Act. On the other hand, with regard to the names of certain municipal activities, there is a need for terminology with the Law on the Transport of Road Transport and the Water Act. In order to address the problems and shortcomings mentioned above, it is necessary to draw up and adopt a new law which will result from the following consequences to establish a system of municipal economy, in line with the laws governing water supply and disposal, waste management, mortuary activity, public procurement, concessions and provision of services, and with specific laws governing issues of the municipal economy. The question of the importance of the municipal economy for living and working in units of local self-government and the principles on which the municipal economy must be based will be clearly and properly addressed. Standard conditions will be provided to improve the implementation of the municipal order in the area of the local self-government unit. It will allow easy and rapid recording of the cataster and entry into land books, municipal infrastructures, built until the new Law on the Communist Economy enters into force. The text of the Law on the municipal economy will become reviewed and easy to apply the implementation, thus improving the effectiveness of its implementation. Furthermore, between the 2 readings, i.e. the debate which was conducted during the first examination of the draft law, the observations and the proposals presented in the debate, as well as the reasons why the law was adopted, this final proposal of the Law on the Communist Economy, which differs significantly from the proposed law, was drawn up in the following. As part of carrying out municipal activities and maintaining municipal infrastructure, it is also possible to ensure the construction of municipal infrastructure, the maintenance of parts of public roads passing through the settlement, has been deleted as a municipal activity. The operation of chimneys and the maintenance of deforestation buildings is defined as a communal activity. ... the previous consent of the minister, as a condition for declaring new municipal activities and closer identification of jobs, which are considered jobs, carried out in the context of municipal activities. [[The speaker doesn't understand.]] Non-profit associations of citizens have been disabled in carrying out the municipal activities of maintaining the cemetery and the activities of the funeral service of the deceased. They've been wiped out like unnecessary rules about jobs that can't be interrupted. It was deleted from a provision which prohibited a commercial company carrying out a municipal activity at the same time as other commercial activities, with other activities carried out in the public interest being required to carry out separate accounting operations. It is deleted as an unnecessary obligation of a company carrying out its municipal activity, to submit to the representative body of the unit of the local self-government of the labour plan, the work report and the financial report. In units of local self-government that have a single administrative department, it is possible to set up their own drive, as an organisational unit in that part or as a special design unit, depending on the needs and wishes of units of local self-government. The deadlines on which a concession for the exercise of municipal activities may be granted have been deleted. The Institute of Public Activity Contracts is fully in line with public procurement rules, and it is impossible to change the legal status of municipal infrastructure by special laws. It is prescribed that the storage and construction of the intended production of the municipal contribution solution is not made by the power of the building permit, but by the power of the use of the permit. The release from payment of certain auxiliary buildings, on a building particle or within the scope of the procedure in the existing building or on the existing building, is restricted only to those buildings intended for use in the main building. The exemption from payment of a municipal contribution for buildings and works that are built without a building permit and gl according to a special regulation has been deleted. the project. The possibility of exemption from payment of the municipal contribution to specific laws has been deleted. A representative body of a unit of local self-government shall be allowed to prescribe general conditions and reasons for which, in individual cases, a partial or full exemption from payment of a municipal contribution is granted. The saying is that garage and business space within the military building and building land within the military site is not considered a garage residential space, and building land for which a municipal fee is charged. A clearer issue has been addressed by the payer of the municipal compensation at the time of its payment of the payment obligations of the payer of the same. There is a limit from the applicable Law on the municipal economy, according to which for hotels, apartment settlements, campsites, the height of the annual municipal fee cannot exceed 1.5% of the total annual income from the previous year achieved in hotels, apartment settlements, camps, which are located in the area of the local self-government unit. The powers of the municipal director in implementing the decision on municipal order have been reduced, a deadline has been extended to establish the register of municipal infrastructure, with 6 months. on a yearly basis, and the text legally technical and spellingly completed. We conclude that the proposed law will meet its objectives and improve the municipal economy in our country. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89197	Dear colleagues and colleagues. At the 20th session held on 14 June 2018, the Committee on Spatial Planning and Construction of the Croatian Parliament discussed the final proposal of the Law on the municipal economy, second reading, P.Z. No. 264, which has been delivered by the Government of the Republic of Croatia to the President of the Croatian Parliament by an act of 7 June 2018. On the basis of Art. 93. The Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament discussed the above act as a parent working body. The proposer's representative pointed out that the proposed Law regulates the principles on which the municipal economy rests, the pursuit of municipal activities, construction and maintenance of infrastructure, payment of contributions and compensation, maintenance of order and other significant issues. The law regulates the types and organisational forms in which municipal activities are carried out, issues related to the performance of these activities, and issues of municipal contribution and compensation. The proposed Law will ensure the standard conditions for improving the implementation of the municipal order in the field of units of local self-government and will allow for the simple recording of municipal infrastructure built up until the entry into force of this Act into the catastro and entry into the land books. Furthermore, differences have been made between the final draft laws in relation to the first reading proposal of the Law. A significant difference from the proposal for the Law in the first reading is that, as part of carrying out municipal activities, the maintenance of municipal infrastructure allows for the provision and construction of municipal infrastructure. Furthermore, maintenance of parts of public roads passing through the settlement has been wiped out as a communal activity. The operation of chimneys and the maintenance of deforestation buildings is defined as a communal activity. It is prescribed that the municipal contribution is not paid by the units of local self-government in their area and that it is not paid for the construction and legalisation of firehouses, military buildings, transport, energy, water, maritime, communication and electrical communication infrastructures, ground and underground production and waterways, as well as sports and children's playgrounds. Next, it is impossible for non-profit associations of citizens to carry out municipal activities, to maintain cemetery and the activities of the burial service of the deceased, the deadline for setting up public infrastructure records has been extended, the powers of the communal director have been reduced in implementing the decision on municipal order and others. In the debate, part of the Committee's members assessed that the proposed solutions in Article 1(2) were questionable. 59, 70, 92, 1st and 5th and 97th. Final proposal of the Law on the municipal economy in relation to Article. 84, Article 2 of the opinion has been expressed that the solution whereby a solution on a municipal contribution is subject to the power of use of the applicable authorisation could slow down the development of economic areas in smaller areas. During the debate, the proposing representatives answered the questions asked and explained the proposed solution in detail. 84.v. 2. which refers to the power of use, to the power of use of the storage permit or to the building of the intended production. The proposed provision is a compromise solution whereby units of local self-government make a contribution to competitiveness and redistribution of entrepreneurship. On the question raised in the debate on municipal directors and their powers, the applicant explained that no new business or material legal authority has been awarded to municipal directors, but only formally prescribed their powers in relation to the lack of the number of municipal directors in rural municipalities, which is highlighted in the debate. According to the proposed law, several units of local self-government can jointly establish municipal redistribution. At its meeting, the proposing representative agreed and accepted the proposed amendments to the Committee on the final proposal of this Act. After the debate, the Committee decided to propose, by a majority of votes, 8 votes in favour, 2 abstentions to the Croatian Parliament, the adoption of the Law on the Communist Economy with the following amendments. Amendment 1 to Article 1 25. We are, in fact, losing a roof Law on the municipal economy that should essentially solve fundamental things, which are principles on which municipal activities are carried out, to list all relevant municipal activities carried out by local self-government units, to define ways in which these activities can be carried out and to define the revenues by which and the ways in which these municipal activities are carried out and financed. It should be the subject of roof law. What is unfortunately happening, and it will deepen with the adoption of this law, if the applicant insists on it, we will have several different regimes of municipal activity. That one part of the municipal activities will remain in this round-cut Law on the municipal economy, and the other part will be singled out in certain specific laws that will prescribe different regimes and functionings and principles and methods of carrying out these municipal activities and financing, etc. And that's not good. What we have also proposed in the proposal, and further, that this law contains all the municipal activities that were originally communal, which today local self-government units are carrying out as municipal activities, and not that we have only one part, and in fact we have singled out others as public water supply or public waste or municipal waste management or some other activities that are vital, absolutely essential in the municipal activities that we have singled out in specific laws and in a special regime that is in fact significantly deviating from the general regime of municipal activities and the principles according to which they should take place. I am responsible for the reasons why some suggestions were not accepted by the applicant, in such a way that some activities are not currently listed in the current Law on the Communist Economy. Among other examples, public water supply or public disposal. These are two extremely important municipal activities that are now listed in the Law on the Communist Economy, and we are proposing them again. They cannot be singled out in some special regime that will no longer be referred to as the municipal sector, the municipal activity, and which may be diminished by amendments to the special laws, that common section, that public service we always identify with municipal activities and that will gradually transform into something else that, through concessions and otherwise, will become more and more private activity that will eventually be privatised through special laws, and we are sharply against it. And that's why we insist that all communal activities be listed in the Law on the Communist Economy, everything. And all those that have been mentioned so far, there is no excuse that, for example, the most important among them are public water supply, municipal waste management, public disposal and wastewater treatment are not in the Law on the municipal economy. These activities were originally in the draft laws that the ministry had just given for public debate, and then removed them from the bill, they are not even in the final proposal, and they even assure us that these activities are not listed in the current law, and they are. The greatest certainty that these activities are not privatised one day is to be defined in this Law on the municipal economy as municipal activities carried out as public service, which are under the important authority of the units of local self-government, is the greatest guarantee. See what's going on in the municipal waste management sector since we haven't defined it as a communal activity and what problems local self-government units have because it's no longer a communal activity, and deep down it is. One of those that's vital to every citizen in any local self-government unit. Of course, we will insist on that in the amendments. Furthermore, there are a number of important provisions that have not been improved in this final bill, some of which we want to highlight. For example, we find it completely unclear why the applicant, without any justification from the legal provision that speaks of concessions, has provided a time limit on which the concession can be awarded. So, even in the current law, you have a deadline that is 30 years long, in the bill you had 10 to 20 years, depending on the municipal activity you want to trust through the concession, and then the promoter, without any reason, removes the deadline and you do not now have a period of concession in this final proposal. I saw the concession law as a roof law that I was convinced might define some sort of general term of the concession. However, in Article 17 of the Treaty on the Functioning of The concession law does not have that time limit, but the law says that each specific activity should be defined by the term of validity of the concession. So, this final bill of law has happened big, but I would say intentional failure. Because if you leave out the period of validity of the concession, that means that the representative body could set a concession for an undefined number of years. This is why we propose an amendment for a maximum period of 10 years. I mention that in one law on sustainable waste management you have in art. It seems to me to me 31, which defines the performance of these tasks, the possibility of awarding a concession by a representative body for a maximum period of 10 years. Therefore, a special law defining the municipal activity of waste management or the collection of municipal waste defines a concession with a maximum period of 10 years, and the roof Law on the municipal economy has eliminated that provision from the proposal of the Law. And you have another slightly worse provision than that, and that's also indicative that a deadline has been left out for which you can enter into a public trust agreement. Therefore, if it is not a concession, you can also entrust a contract for a certain period of time to some exercise of municipal activity. In the current Law on the municipal economy, the deadline is 4 years. So, it's one term of office of the representative body, including the executive body, and that trust agreement was, of course, passed by the representative body, and that's the solution we had in the Bill's proposal. So the bill of law did not deviate from the existing solution that we have in the existing Law on the Communist Economy. And what happens in the final proposal? In your final proposal, you have two changes. The first is that, instead of a representative body, the mayor, or the municipal chief, is determined to enter into a contract to entrust municipal affairs and the second, even worse, the deadline on which such a contract can be concluded is deleted. Therefore, the final proposal, without any justification, is given the authority of one person under the law, undefined and unlimited period, to conclude a contract for the performance of municipal transactions, with no justification whatsoever. Of course, we have put an amendment to this provision to bring it back to the original in the Bill's proposal, which is that the contract is concluded by a representative body, so it makes a decision to accept the confidence of municipal affairs and that the deadline is the longest of four years, as it has always been. And through this Act we want to restore power to the representative body wherever possible, and this is also done by the founding rights of the institutions, to give consent to the prices of municipal services and to change the prices of municipal services. And that says enough. ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89405	We move to the next item of the agenda, that is: - Proposal for a law on the acquisition and possession of weapons of citizens, first reading, P.Z.E. No. 364; Proposer is the Government of the Republic of Croatia pursuant to Article 85. The Constitution of the Republic of Croatia and Article 172. Rules of Procedure of the Croatian Parliament. When discussing this item of the agenda, the provisions of the Rules of Procedure relating to first reading of the Law shall apply. The debate was conducted by the Committee on Law, the Committee on Internal Policy and National Security. Would the applicant's representative like to give an additional explanation? Here you go. We have Mr. Zarko Katic, State Secretary at the Ministry of Interior. Here you go.
ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89406	Madam MPs, ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament. The proposal of the Law on the supply and supply of arms to citizens is a law which will regulate the procurement and registration of arms and the extension of the period of validity of the arms and ammunition documents, as well as the way in which the citizens are dealt with for civil use, or for the purposes of hunting, shooting or for personal security or self-defense. This draft law also transposes the EU Directive No. 853 of 2017 on the control of the supply and possession of weapons. In fact, this Directive complemented the '91 directive. which was still changed in 2008 and with which our current law was in conformity. The Republic of Croatia currently registered 270 thousand 932 pieces of weapons used for civilian purposes. Of which 184 thousand 219 weapons were registered for hunting purposes, so most for hunting purposes, 5 thousand 143 pieces of weapons for sports shooting and 81 thousand 570 pieces of weapons were registered for personal safety purposes. The age limit for the supply of weapons remains the same, or 21 years, with exceptions for certain categories. These are police officers, judicial officers, members of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Croatia, other state bodies, security guards who are authorised under special regulations to hold and carry arms, members of sports shooting associations actively competing in the shooting and persons with a hunting test capable of obtaining weapons from 18 years of age. In Articles 10 to 20, the law regulates the precise approval procedure for the supply of weapons to a natural person, which distinguishes general conditions and special conditions. General conditions are that a person has a valid reason for the purchase of weapons, therefore, or has passed a hunting test to actively compete in the shooting or has a particular reason for the supply of weapons for personal safety. A person must be 21 years old, as I said, committed, except for the exceptions listed in the law, that no criminal or criminal proceedings have been brought against him, or that he is legally convicted of certain, precisely numbered crimes, the most serious crimes and violations with the elements of violence and that he has secured conditions for safe accommodation and weapons storage. Where such conditions are met by a person, then specific conditions concerning the health status of the applicant are required, so that the citizen has technical knowledge and skills for the proper use of the weapon and that he knows the rules relating to the possession of the weapon. When all these conditions have been met, the citizen shall be granted authorisation for the purchase of a weapon with which he has a period of 6 months to obtain a specific weapon and then request the release of the arms list with that weapon. With this bill, weapons are classified into four categories. Category A where the weapon that can be registered and thus be legally owned in category B is a weapon in the prior procurement authorisation regime that I was talking about, in category C, the firearms and other weapons that need to be reported to the competent authority, therefore the PU or the D-cell and category D in which the arms and devices do not need to be reported to the competent authority after the purchase. This categorisation is based on the directive and it is only in some cases more stringent than the directive. In several cases, we went harder than the directive, for example, some category of firearms could be placed in category C, which should be subsequently reported, but we decided that the concept of all firearms should be in category B with prior procurement approval. Otherwise, the bill simplifies the procedure to be carried out for the purpose of requesting citizens to obtain firearms and will no longer carry out operational verifications carried out by police officers on the ground or in talks with neighbours and other persons known to the applicant and thus attempt to establish the existence of circumstances that indicate possible weapons abuse. In fact, we believe that experience has shown us that such tests often did not bring any added value, and they have significantly exhausted the work of police officers on the ground and took away the time they could use to carry out some other tasks, according to our assessment of more important tasks. I mentioned as a special condition for the supply of arms is the possession of a citizen's health ability and therefore the citizen is obliged to give information about his chosen primary health care doctor, and the PU or the station will inform the selected doctor about all persons who have been issued the gun list. Based on this information, the selected primary healthcare physician will inform the PU or become aware of a change in the health status of the gun owner that affects its ability to own weapons of course. The citizen, as proposed by this Law, will not have to go to medical examinations as it has been every 5 years, but health assurance will therefore contribute to the purchase of weapons and afterwards from 70 years of age. So within 15 days of 70 years of his life, he will have to deliver a conviction that he is still healthy and capable of having weapons. After that, every five years they will have to adapt that belief of health skills. The proposal of the Law is extremely permitted to use children over 9 years of age with weapons of category B, as well as air weapons and weapons with tendons, and until now they were only 11 years older, so that they competed in sports competitions only in the places designated for these competitions and arranged for training and under the supervision of coaches. The age limit has been reduced because on a proposal from the Croatian Shooting Union, it has been established that our younger age categories of sports shooters achieve worse results than competitors from other countries in which they are allowed to engage in this sport at an earlier age. We therefore coordinate this with most other countries. In addition, the proposal for a law requires that exceptional weapons of category B should be used by minors aged 17 and 6 months in training for a hunter also under the supervision of persons authorised to perform a programme for training hunters and with the consent of parents or guardians. The directive I spoke about explicitly allows the use of firearms for sports and hunting and persons under 18 years of age if they have the consent of parents or are controlled by an adult with a valid permit to carry firearms or are located within a licensed or otherwise authorised training centre. In doing so, we have compared the situation in other EU countries and most of these countries have, therefore, allowed younger people to train for a hunter, under the supervision of an authorised person and with the consent of their parents. Only that weapons are rarely used in the proper use of injuries, but supervision of the installation of weapons against persons using it is extremely important and although certain procedures have been simplified, it remains an emphasis on the safe use of firearms. Still in art. In 79, the possibility of returning weapons that are illegally owned by citizens is prescribed and for the past 10 years, the action of the MUP "less weapons, less tragedy" in which citizens are called for the voluntary surrender of illegal weapons, so that in the last 10 years, citizens have handed over over 4.5 thousand pieces of automatic firearms, over 316 thousand of which are spraying weapons, i.e. bombs, grenades or the like. 4.8 million rounds of ammunition and another four and a half tons of different explosives. Also during that period, 7762 were handed over to allow firearms that were not protested in a valid, correct manner. So they didn't have permission to hold or carry that weapon. Equally every year, a number of weapons are withdrawn during administrative proceedings, criminal or criminal proceedings. For example, in the last year, a total of 5292 with firearms of which 2215 were seized in administrative proceedings, 2850 who were in criminal and 119 who were in criminal proceedings. Here, these are the basic points of the law that lies before you in the first reading, all your remarks, suggestions and suggestions will be carefully considered and, if necessary, included in the text of the final bill. Thank you very much for your attention.

ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89426	Thank you, Mr. Vice President. Your Honor, Secretary of State. Here is the Law on the acquisition and registration of weapons that introduces in us an unnecessary new category of weapons, category D, which even the very strict EU Directive of 2017 does not know. There is no rational explanation for such service. This will create a problem for many EU citizens who come to Croatia recreationally because almost all EU countries do not have this category D, so, for example, at the borders, they will have surprising problems due to the transfer of practically R soft and paintball toys. It will be the subject of ridicule and embezzlement of tourists and of presenting unnecessary complexity and administration of persons who use R software and imitations for entertainment and play in nature and who in no way have the possibility with such R software reproductions to endanger life. The law generally misdefined the collection of weapons and went beyond the eu directive. It is surprising that the Ministry of Culture has missed the fact that national historical and cultural treasures will be destroyed in ancient iron by many natural persons who have spent enormous resources and efforts to collect and record. We suggest that a few things change in the law. In Article 6(6), the term placed in the context of school ammunition should be deleted because it has been deactivated with a more general importance. In fact, the school ammunition is not deactivated by ammunition but by purposely designed ammunition for showing purposes, special structures other than deactivated and normally filled with plastic or other mass. In practice, it will cause confusion because a large amount of deactivated ammunition is owned by natural and legal persons and serve as decorations, locks for keys, pens, etc. Article 6 paragraph 20 where a sentence is defined and which cannot be converted to fire a shotgun, grains or missiles by means of an incendiary thrust because it is a provision which, in the nature of all the signal guns that the seamen legally have to put at the edge of the law. In fact, every pipe is in theory, and it is possible to remanufactuate the water line to fire the shotgun, and this regulation is an over-normation, and it will create a problem especially in maritime-oriented areas where chaos and interpretation problems will arise. Article 6(6), where the concept of firearms is defined, the word behind the word can be inserted, added by a simple technical process, given that in the theory of each piece of weapon, each air rifle, each pipe with a handle is converted into a weapon. However, such modifications require complex technical procedures which are not available to persons without specific technical and weapons skills and do not have special machines for this purpose. It is therefore necessary to access clearer and more limited definition of firearms. In Article 7 of point 12, it is proposed to delete the text of silencers intended for firearms and parts for silencers, since developed Western countries have realised that the silence of shooting is particularly desirable in hunting for the health of hunters, shooters and for environmental reasons as it reduces noise in the environment. Several Scandinavian northern countries have introduced silencers as a preferred practice in hunting Germany, Finland, Denmark, Great Britain, for them are issued purchasing licences as well as for weapons. These countries have properly introduced the registration of silencers, shootings and long-term practices have proved to be very positive, so that part of the text should be discarded and allowed to purchase a silencer. The ELF Directive in Article 6(2) provides that the educational, cultural, research and historical purposes and without prejudice to Article 6(1) of that Directive, the competent national authorities may, in an exceptional and duly justified manner, issue authorisations for firearms, essential parts and ammunition in category A where this is not contrary to public security or public order. This means that the EU has allowed in nature that collectors who are supporters and conservationists of cultural and historical heritage can possess incapacitated weapons of A category and its parts. But this bill does not define it in a quality way, denying citizens that possibility and forcing them to destroy such heritage as forcing culture. If that were the case, Article 6(3) of the Directive states that Member States may issue to collectors authorisations for the supply and possession of essential parts and ammunition classified in category A subject to strict safety conditions. The only reason why this law does not approach collectors with attention is because of the laziness of a state apparatus that would rather be allowed to destroy national treasures than have to have one more thing on the record. It is surprising that the Ministry of Culture had no objection to this part of the text that represents the path of destroying the sows of Croatian and European military history. In Article 7, paragraph 3 is proposed to add point 17, which is the silencer intended for the weapon. This is because the 2017 Directive does not treat silencers as part of weapons and is not mentioned in the same question, based on what data, studies, research has concluded that the silencers are a category of weapons together with automatic weapons, rocket launchers, etc. Such claims speak of a high level of ignorance and misunderstanding of the function and purpose of the silencer. Trends worldwide and in the EU are encouraging the use of silencers in sports shooters, hunters and other users of firearms because the positive impact of silencers in protecting health, people and the environment is recognised. The silencers are devices that are mounted at the end of the gun pipe and help reduce noise levels due to gunshots to values that are safe for people's health. The silencers are essentially nothing more than a simple pipe with a series of bulkheads that help both retain and reroute the gunpowder gases that come out of the pipe after failure. According to their performance and purpose, they are very similar to the exhaust system in the car and are simultaneously invented and developed in the early 1900s by the same man. Using a silencer to use weapons can make it safer, more pleasant and acceptable for other people in the environment, e.g. Inhabitants near the shooting range, hunting grounds. Hearing damage caused by exposure to excessive noise is a serious problem in today’s world. Many of the people who have suffered hearing damage have been exposed to gunshots from firearms. This applies mainly to sportsmen, hunters, cops, soldiers, security guards and all those who use firearms as well as those who are close to using them. The silencer can help prevent hearing damage as recognised in some EU countries and beyond where the intervention of silencers is made possible by citizens and even encouraged by the state. The silencer is especially useful for hunters when it is not practical to have ear protection when hunting. In hunting, hunters do not wear antiphones because they listen to the environment and dogs and communicate with each other. Again, not a muffled shot is loud enough to cause permanent hearing damage. The use of silencers will significantly reduce the level of noise resulting from gunshots to protect hearing without using the antiphone. It will also reduce the level of stress in dogs that are also exposed to discomfort. The use of silencers will reduce any discomfort of citizens if the hunting takes place near the settlements and possibly avoid unpleasant situations. For hunters, it is also important that the use of the silencer reduces the trigger of the weapon, removes the blast waves of the blast, reduces the bounce of the weapon, after which it is faster and easier to aim after the first shooting, i.e. The target's coming back on target faster. The use of silencers on the range range except for hearing, user and other people staying on and around the range is useful because of a decrease in noise levels that often bother the surrounding tenants and cause discomfort. It is known that there are shooting sites located near settlements or cities, and there are always comments by residents on noise caused by firearms shootings. Using silencers helps to ease complaints against the noise of the surrounding tenants and make co-existence easier. Suffocators do not increase crime, some people believe that by allowing the possession of silencers for civilians to increase crime. That's completely wrong. According to a study done in the United States, which is the first in the world by number of weapons per capita and where the possession of silencers has been allowed in 42 countries of 50, it has been concluded that silencers have rarely been used in criminal acts. The studies looked at the period from 1995 to 2005 in California and other countries and found that the silencer was used in less than 0.1 criminal activities. The silencer is obviously not a source of crime because he does not actually muffle the shot as in the movies, he cannot use the guns, he prevents the normal operation of the vast majority of semi-automatic pistols unless a special device is added to enable him to build a plunger, makes the weapon longer and harder to manipulate and hide. Some of the countries where the possession of silencers for civilian use are allowed are Denmark, Finland, France, Germany, Sweden, Great Britain, Ukraine, USA, New Zealand, etc. In the case of a Member State of the European Union, the Commission shall adopt delegated acts in accordance with the procedure laid down in this Article and shall adopt delegated acts in accordance with the procedure laid down in this Article. 8. For the sake of clarity of defining that cold weapons in the form of bayonets and daggers in the collector do not pose a danger, it is useful to put behind the words of category A cold weapons whose primary purpose is to attack or inflict injuries, add text, and the ways and circumstances of their wear indicate that they are worn to be used for assault or injury. It is proposed to delete the Article. To the wheel. 4. words, with the exception of devices designed for R soft and paintball, which should be reported in case of crossing the state border because it is an inappropriate prenormisation that will cause problems for tourists and foreigners, as this substance is very rarely defined in other EU countries in view of their entry into another country. Here, these are our remarks, so here's hoping you'll respect them. Thank you very much.

This Proposal for a Law regulates the procedures for the procurement of weapons registration and the extension of the period of validity of the arms documents, as well as the way in which weapons and ammunition are handled by citizens for civil use. The matter contained in the applicable Arms Act relating to the production and transport of weapons, the intermediation of arms, the repair and processing of weapons, the transport of weapons, the testing and marking of the brand of firearms and civilian ammunition was regulated by the Law on Explosive Substances, and the production and transport of weapons with which the provisions of the Arms Act governing that substance ceased to apply. According to the Bill's proposal, the age limit for the purchase of arms is 21 years, with the exception of the police officers of the MUP, members of the OSR, the judicial police, other state authorities and security guards who are authorised to hold and carry arms under special regulations, members of the Sports Shooting Association who are actively competing in shooting and persons with a hunting test capable of obtaining weapons from 18 years of age. Weapons can be obtained for hunting, sports shooting or personal security or self-defense. The proposal for a law simplifies and speeds up the procedure to be applied for by citizens for the authorisation of the supply of firearms. Also, police officers will not in the living rooms of a person who purchases a weapon check that the person has secured the conditions for safe installation and weapons storage, but the person will make a statement of this under material and criminal responsibility when applying for the supply of the weapons and, if subsequently, appropriate actions will be carried out against it and the weapons will be taken away. This way, speaking of weapons accommodation and storage, I again remind you of the disappearance of a weapon from a police warehouse in Varazdin. For example, there are silencers, bombs, explosives, laser targets, from which it is clear that the weapon was not intended, for example, for poaching. I appeal to do everything, but everything, to find it. This bill provides for categorisation, firearms, which is in line with the categorisation set out in Directive 7017/853. In category A, firearms were classified, such as explosive military missiles, launchers, all types of weapons with silencer, etc. which is prohibited for civilian use. In category B, firearms requiring authorisation for supply and category C in which firearms, other weapons, which are to be declared after purchase by the competent police administration or the Home Affairs Ministry station, with stricter conditions for the categorisation of firearms, i.e. certain types of firearms, which are classified in the C category by the proposal of this law, are classified in the B category. The bill also prescribed a D category of weapons and devices that do not need to be reported to the competent police office or station after the procurement, but some of them just need to report to the border police, in case of a transfer across the state border. Furthermore, in the purchase of weapons, the citizen will have to attach his confidence in the medical ability to possess weapons, but after that, he won't have to go for a health check every 5 years as before. The next medical examination will have to be carried out when he turns 70, namely, on the issue of authorisation for the supply of weapons, will be notified of his forced health care, which will have to inform the police station of any change in the health status or course of treatment of the gun owner, which affects the medical ability to possess weapons. However, the police administration or the station will, every 5 years on official duty, verify whether the owner of the weapons meets the conditions of weapons possession and by taking into account the official records of the competent authorities of the state administration. It should be noted that this proposal for a law will remove the observed shortcomings in the previous law, which concern shortcomings in the implementation of administrative procedures, which are carried out in connection with requests for the supply and registration of weapons, and will also more clearly regulate the supply and use of weapons for civil use. Following all of this, the HDZ Representatives Club will support the draft law in its first reading. Thank you.
