ParlaMint-HR_2018-06-28-0.u89517	Dear Vice-President of the Croatian Parliament, respected colleagues and fellow MPs. So the government of the Republic of Croatia proposes to the Croatian Parliament not to accept the conclusions of the interpretation of the work of the Government of the Republic of Croatia to act on the announced renationalisation of the INA and privatisation of 25% of the less than one of the shares of HEP. Oil industries d.d. in the Republic of Croatia has a long tradition, has a significant role in oil business in Croatia, as well as in the region in the exploration, production of oil and gas, oil processing and distribution of oil and petroleum products. The Government of the Republic of Croatia therefore considers that, due to the great importance of the INA, the Croatian economy as a whole, it is necessary to maintain its business in a way that buys from MOL the ownership stake in order for the Republic of Croatia to acquire majority ownership of INA and thereby restore management rights and thus control and control of INA. Since the proposer of this interpretation uses the term renationalisation of the company INA d.d. In this context, the Government considers it necessary to point out that it is not a renationalisation, but rather a buy-in procedure that is common in the process of changing the ownership structure of any company, whether it is a commercial company owned by a state or other natural or legal persons. It is evident that INA’s business is in a continuous decline year after year, and its market position is systematically devastationed. The proposer himself in the interpretation text cites a series of data that speak for it. Total revenues decreased by 37%, the amount of processed oil decreased by around 21%, the target decreased by 60%. All business indicators show a devastating management policy between 2012 and 2015, i.e. precisely in the government's mandate headed by the SDP at the time. This clearly shows the complete absence of any prudent and determined policy and the will of the Government then to take any measures to change the negative indicators through the available mechanisms and then existing trends in INA operations. In other words, the most responsible for the INA's current position is the Government from 2012 to 2015. Another important reason for the change in the ownership structure of the INA is the fact that only one shareholder decides in decision-making procedures, i.e. to ignore the interests of other shareholders and, above all, the strategic interest of the Republic of Croatia. Aware of the importance of INA for oil business in Croatia and of the need to protect strategic national interest in the area of energy stability and stop these negative trends in INA business, this government has made a decision to buy shares from the Hungarian MOL. The government's decision to buy the MOL, the MOL's stake in INA, as I recall, was supported by the public and the SDP, and you respected gentlemen, almost everyone who proposes this interpretation. On 19 January 2017, the Government set up a negotiating advice with Hungary's MOL whose task was to give guidance, take action and activities and propose to the Government the best model for the purchase of INA shares held today by MOL. However, given the fact that one of the objectives of this government is to reduce public debt, which is supported by the government's adoption of a public debt management strategy for 2017-'19. In the year, the government of the Republic of Croatia has made it clear that one of the key elements in selecting the INA stock buyout model is, and that this model does not cause an increase in public debt. The government's ultimate goal is to ensure economic growth and development, i.e. economic stability, and the government recognises that the energy strategy for which INA is crucial, is one of the most important factors in economic stability. In August of last year, on the basis of a proposal for a negotiating council with the Hungarian MOL, the government concluded that the selection of an investment adviser in the transaction of a possible purchase of INA shares owned by MOL and that the procedure was jointly carried out by the Ministry of Finance and the Ministry of Environmental and Energy Protection. An invitation to tender was sent to the addresses of a total of 22 potential renowned international advisers and 7 offers were received. In April this year, on the basis of an investment advisor selection process, a consortium consisting of Morgan Stanley, Intensa Sao Paulo Group and Privredna banka Zagreb, including due to relevant experience in the analysis of the INA Group's previous operations. The obligation of investment advisers to carry out a deep analysis of the company, assess the value of the INA itself and assist the Government of the Republic of Croatia in finding and selecting potential strategic partners. The position of this government is that INA remains a vertically integrated company with all its components, and in this context they took these activities that I was just talking about. As regards the publication of the decision of the arbitration court and Geneva, I will repeat the government's opinion based on the rules of the Commission on international trade law, that the decision can only be made public in agreement with both parties, and according to procedural orders, all aspects of this arbitration procedure must be kept secret and even after the arbitration procedure has been concluded. In addition, the publication of a decision by this arbitration court could have a negative impact on the arbitration procedure between the Republic of Croatia and MOL before the International Centre for the Resolution of Investment Disputes in Washington. The proponent of interpretation in the conclusions proposes to the Croatian Parliament to oppose the privatisation of HEP for the renationalisation of INA. It should be recalled, however, that the government was aware of all those advances that needed to be done in order to go to HEP's initial public offering at all and yet the first thing it did was to choose a consultant to make initial offers. Therefore, the initial public offer of HEP, which was actually launched during the government of Mr Zoran Milanovic, which included some of the signatories of this impelation, should not be a point of opposition for the promoters of this interpretation. If you were then in favour of reducing the share of ownership of the Republic of Croatia in HEP, because one of your arguments, that was, that the prepared actions you've conducted, pointed out the fact that the electricity companies listed on the stock market are doing better than those companies, which are completely state-owned, raise one key question, why are you opposed to that today? Finally, the Government considers that in order to protect national interests, it is necessary to buy the shares of the INA, implement a comprehensive restructuring of that company, and focus business policy on strengthening activities, which will ensure INA's growth and development and return to it that market position which it had back 10 years to which it actually belongs, and the methodology or model of purchase, after deep analysis, will be proposed to the government by the selected investment advisor after the signing of the contract. Therefore, I once again express the government's position in my first sentence, so the Government of the Republic of Croatia proposes to the Croatian Parliament not to accept the conclusions of this interpretation. Thank you very much.
